for for the reminder, @dcheng1248 and apologizes for not reacting promptly. \n\nI am still pondering whether we should use the \"nameless\" default Quarkus trust store defined by properties `quarkus.tls.key-store.*`, `quarkus.tls.trust-store.*`. etc. as a default trust store for CXF clients. \n\nThat's how they do it in some Quarkus extensions\n\nGRPC client\n\nhttps://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/5534ed16ac3140034f938e2a21a9d753958e6bc8/extensions/grpc/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/grpc/runtime/config/GrpcClientConfiguration.java#L69-L82\n\nOpenTelemetry\n\nhttps://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/5534ed16ac3140034f938e2a21a9d753958e6bc8/extensions/opentelemetry/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/opentelemetry/runtime/config/runtime/exporter/OtlpExporterConfig.java#L100-L112\n\nREST Client\n\nhttps://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/5534ed16ac3140034f938e2a21a9d753958e6bc8/extensions/resteasy-classic/rest-client-config/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/restclient/config/RestClientsConfig.java#L258-L268\n\n\nBut on the other hand, there are extensions not taking `quarkus.tls.*` as a default:\n\nKeycloak admin\n\nhttps://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/5534ed16ac3140034f938e2a21a9d753958e6bc8/extensions/keycloak-admin-client-common/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/keycloak/admin/client/common/KeycloakAdminClientConfig.java#L76-L83\n\nMailer\n\nhttps://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/5534ed16ac3140034f938e2a21a9d753958e6bc8/extensions/mailer/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/mailer/runtime/MailerRuntimeConfig.java#L73-L84\n\nOIDC Client\n\nhttps://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/5534ed16ac3140034f938e2a21a9d753958e6bc8/extensions/oidc-common/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/oidc/common/runtime/config/OidcCommonConfig.java#L94-L102\n\nRedis Client\n\nhttps://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/5534ed16ac3140034f938e2a21a9d753958e6bc8/extensions/redis-client/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/redis/runtime/client/config/RedisClientConfig.java#L223-L234\n\nWebSocket client\n\nhttps://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/5534ed16ac3140034f938e2a21a9d753958e6bc8/extensions/websockets-next/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/websockets/next/runtime/config/WebSocketsClientRuntimeConfig.java#L55-L63\n\nThere seem to be all kinds of flavors to handle the defaults.\n\nI think in our case, a CXF specific client-only property (let's call it `quarkus.cxf.client.tls-configuration-name`) would be useful, so that following use cases are possible: \n\n* Set the TLS for all clients that is different from `quarkus.tls.*` default, because the `quarkus.tls.*` is the default for server side and CXF clients may need a different keystore content.\n* Set the same TLS for all clients as `quarkus.tls.*` - this could be the case either by default or by setting `quarkus.cxf.client.tls-configuration-name = default`\n* Set all CXF clients use the default Java trust store typically found under  `$JAVA_HOME/lib/security/cacerts` to make VertxHttpClientConduit based clients compatible with HttpURLConnectionConduit - this could be the case either by default or by setting `quarkus.cxf.client.tls-configuration-name = java.net.ssl` - see https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/issues/45175 and https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/pull/45184 - still unclear whether this reaches Quarkus 3.18.0\n\n(We need tests for all the above scenarios.)\n\nWe have to decide what would the default of `quarkus.cxf.client.tls-configuration-name`. levels of named and default TLS configs as well as for the old (and now deprecated) `quarkus.cxf.client.\"client-name\".key-store*` and `quarkus.cxf.client.\"client-name\".trust-store` family of I'm still new to large codebases so your examples provide great help for me to orient myself and think about the issue and implementations. \n\nTo your question, here are my thoughts:\n\n- `default` pointing to `quarkus.tls.*`: \nThis would introduce an additional layer of dependency on the TLS registry, although making it align with GRPC and REST could be beneficial. But would this lead to potential (security) issues? I don't understand this completely but remember seeing this in the Zulip chat between you and Clement.\n\n- `java.net.ssl`: \nI believe this provides the most drop-in compatibility with URLConnectionHTTPConduit, as it doesn't require any TLS to be set and works out of the box. However, if `quarkus.tls.*` is set by some users, it could be confusing that the CXF default points to somewhere else entirely. Additionally, it seems that none of the existing quarkus extensions use this by default, so it would be a large deviation from existing practices. \n\n- none\nI personally don't see much benefit to this option. Since the `quarkus.cxf.client.\"client-name\".trust-store` etc. are deprecated, a named TLS configuration needs to be set anyway, and having some sort of default is potentially less work/repeated code for users than having none?\n\nI'm tempted towards the second option just for the compatibility, but think the first could also be a good choice. Do you think it would be practical to throw an error (that asks the user to set the tls configurations) if the user sets a client-endpoint-url to https but do not have a tls configured? This would prevent going down the rabbit hole of weird server responses (like I did) due to the lack of TLS. If this is possible, I would be inclined to say the first option along with the error warning. \n\nFor the implementation, I would love to contribute. How urgent do you think this issue is? I will have a lot more time starting next week, as I will be on holiday from work. This is, however, an admittedly non-trivial implementation for me (due to unfamiliarity with the codebase, needing to think about the precedence rules and my general inexperience) so it might take me a bit longer. If you prefer a faster implementation, I would love to help however I can. think `quarkus.tls.*` sounds like the best fit for storing the key for the HTTP server whereas `java.net.ssl` would generally make the best out of the box experience for clients that do not need mTLS. Compatibility with `URLConnectionHTTPConduit` is another strong argument for taking `java.net.ssl` as a default for `quarkus.cxf.client.tls-configuration-name`. So let's do it in that way. \n\n\u003e Do you think it would be practical to throw an error (that asks the user to set the tls configurations) if the user sets a client-endpoint-url to https but do not have a tls configured?\n\nWould we need it if we make `java.net.ssl` the default?\n\n\u003e For the implementation, I would love to contribute. How urgent do you think this issue is?\n\nNot urgent from my PoV, but folks following https://github.com/quarkiverse/quarkus-cxf/issues/1665 might be happy if it could reach \n3.19.0 that we'll release [around Feb. 19th ](https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/wiki/Release-Planning#319-proposal).\n\nLet me assign it to you for now. Feel free to let me know if you stop looking at it. In our use case, we have all the certificates gathered into one truststore but have several clients, and it can feel cumbersome to have to set the property for every client. actions</span></div></div></div></div></div><div class="IssueBody__1iGjHw" id="issue-body-viewer" data-testid="issue-body-viewer"><div data-testid="markdown-body" data-team-hovercards-enabled="true" class="markdown-body" data-turbolinks="false"><div class="markdown-body safe-html-box__Uwc6Al Container__ZAxOBo"><p dir="auto">Currently, to set the TLS truststore for a client, the quarkus property <code class="notranslate">quarkus.cxf.client."client-name".tls-configuration-name</code> must be set. In our use case, we have all the certificates gathered into one truststore but have several clients, and it can feel cumbersome to have to set the property for every client. It would help avoid repeated code if an app-wide property is introduced.</p>
<p dir="auto">I would be happy to see if I can help implement this if you think it is worth doing but not high priority.</p></div></div><div class="IssueBodyTaskList__-FlQre"><div role="toolbar" aria-label="Reactions" class="d-flex gap-1 flex-wrap"></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></div><!--$--><div data-testid="issue-viewer-comments-container" class="react-comments-container"><div class="Body__scgEjp IssueCommentBody__DGWd7U"><div data-testid="markdown-body" data-team-hovercards-enabled="true" class="markdown-body" data-turbolinks="false"><div class="markdown-body safe-html-box__Uwc6Al Container__ZAxOBo"><p dir="auto">Thanks for for the reminder, <a class="user-mention notranslate" data-hovercard-type="user" data-hovercard-url="/users/dcheng1248/hovercard" data-octo-click="hovercard-link-click" data-octo-dimensions="link_type:self" href="https://github.com/dcheng1248">@dcheng1248</a> and apologizes for not reacting promptly.</p>
<p dir="auto">I am still pondering whether we should use the "nameless" default Quarkus trust store defined by properties <code class="notranslate">quarkus.tls.key-store.*</code>, <code class="notranslate">quarkus.tls.trust-store.*</code>. etc. as a default trust store for CXF clients.</p>
<p dir="auto">That's how they do it in some Quarkus extensions</p>
<p dir="auto">GRPC client</p>
<p dir="auto"><a href="https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/5534ed16ac3140034f938e2a21a9d753958e6bc8/extensions/grpc/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/grpc/runtime/config/GrpcClientConfiguration.java#L69-L82">https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/5534ed16ac3140034f938e2a21a9d753958e6bc8/extensions/grpc/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/grpc/runtime/config/GrpcClientConfiguration.java#L69-L82</a></p>
<p dir="auto">OpenTelemetry</p>
<p dir="auto"><a href="https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/5534ed16ac3140034f938e2a21a9d753958e6bc8/extensions/opentelemetry/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/opentelemetry/runtime/config/runtime/exporter/OtlpExporterConfig.java#L100-L112">https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/5534ed16ac3140034f938e2a21a9d753958e6bc8/extensions/opentelemetry/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/opentelemetry/runtime/config/runtime/exporter/OtlpExporterConfig.java#L100-L112</a></p>
<p dir="auto">REST Client</p>
<p dir="auto"><a href="https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/5534ed16ac3140034f938e2a21a9d753958e6bc8/extensions/resteasy-classic/rest-client-config/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/restclient/config/RestClientsConfig.java#L258-L268">https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/5534ed16ac3140034f938e2a21a9d753958e6bc8/extensions/resteasy-classic/rest-client-config/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/restclient/config/RestClientsConfig.java#L258-L268</a></p>
<p dir="auto">But on the other hand, there are extensions not taking <code class="notranslate">quarkus.tls.*</code> as a default:</p>
<p dir="auto">Keycloak admin</p>
<p dir="auto"><a href="https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/5534ed16ac3140034f938e2a21a9d753958e6bc8/extensions/keycloak-admin-client-common/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/keycloak/admin/client/common/KeycloakAdminClientConfig.java#L76-L83">https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/5534ed16ac3140034f938e2a21a9d753958e6bc8/extensions/keycloak-admin-client-common/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/keycloak/admin/client/common/KeycloakAdminClientConfig.java#L76-L83</a></p>
<p dir="auto">Mailer</p>
<p dir="auto"><a href="https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/5534ed16ac3140034f938e2a21a9d753958e6bc8/extensions/mailer/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/mailer/runtime/MailerRuntimeConfig.java#L73-L84">https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/5534ed16ac3140034f938e2a21a9d753958e6bc8/extensions/mailer/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/mailer/runtime/MailerRuntimeConfig.java#L73-L84</a></p>
<p dir="auto">OIDC Client</p>
<p dir="auto"><a href="https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/5534ed16ac3140034f938e2a21a9d753958e6bc8/extensions/oidc-common/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/oidc/common/runtime/config/OidcCommonConfig.java#L94-L102">https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/5534ed16ac3140034f938e2a21a9d753958e6bc8/extensions/oidc-common/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/oidc/common/runtime/config/OidcCommonConfig.java#L94-L102</a></p>
<p dir="auto">Redis Client</p>
<p dir="auto"><a href="https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/5534ed16ac3140034f938e2a21a9d753958e6bc8/extensions/redis-client/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/redis/runtime/client/config/RedisClientConfig.java#L223-L234">https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/5534ed16ac3140034f938e2a21a9d753958e6bc8/extensions/redis-client/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/redis/runtime/client/config/RedisClientConfig.java#L223-L234</a></p>
<p dir="auto">WebSocket client</p>
<p dir="auto"><a href="https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/5534ed16ac3140034f938e2a21a9d753958e6bc8/extensions/websockets-next/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/websockets/next/runtime/config/WebSocketsClientRuntimeConfig.java#L55-L63">https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/blob/5534ed16ac3140034f938e2a21a9d753958e6bc8/extensions/websockets-next/runtime/src/main/java/io/quarkus/websockets/next/runtime/config/WebSocketsClientRuntimeConfig.java#L55-L63</a></p>
<p dir="auto">There seem to be all kinds of flavors to handle the defaults.</p>
<p dir="auto">I think in our case, a CXF specific client-only property (let's call it <code class="notranslate">quarkus.cxf.client.tls-configuration-name</code>) would be useful, so that following use cases are possible:</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li>Set the TLS for all clients that is different from <code class="notranslate">quarkus.tls.*</code> default, because the <code class="notranslate">quarkus.tls.*</code> is the default for server side and CXF clients may need a different keystore content.</li>
<li>Set the same TLS for all clients as <code class="notranslate">quarkus.tls.*</code> - this could be the case either by default or by setting <code class="notranslate">quarkus.cxf.client.tls-configuration-name = default</code></li>
<li>Set all CXF clients use the default Java trust store typically found under  <code class="notranslate">$JAVA_HOME/lib/security/cacerts</code> to make VertxHttpClientConduit based clients compatible with HttpURLConnectionConduit - this could be the case either by default or by setting <code class="notranslate">quarkus.cxf.client.tls-configuration-name = java.net.ssl</code> - see <span class="reference"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-issue-closed fgColor-done mr-1" title="Closed" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="M11.28 6.78a.75.75 0 0 0-1.06-1.06L7.25 8.69 5.78 7.22a.75.75 0 0 0-1.06 1.06l2 2a.75.75 0 0 0 1.06 0l3.5-3.5Z"></path><path d="M16 8A8 8 0 1 1 0 8a8 8 0 0 1 16 0Zm-1.5 0a6.5 6.5 0 1 0-13 0 6.5 6.5 0 0 0 13 0Z"></path></svg><a class="issue-link js-issue-link" data-error-text="Failed to load title" data-id="2745039999" data-permission-text="Title is private" data-url="https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/issues/45175" data-hovercard-type="issue" data-hovercard-url="/quarkusio/quarkus/issues/45175/hovercard" href="https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/issues/45175">TLS Registry: provide a TLS configuration called <code>javax.net.ssl</code> having truststore set in the same way as default SunJSSE provider<span class="issue-shorthand"> quarkusio/quarkus#45175</span></a></span> and <span class="reference"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-git-merge merged fgColor-done mr-1" title="Merged" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="M5.45 5.154A4.25 4.25 0 0 0 9.25 7.5h1.378a2.251 2.251 0 1 1 0 1.5H9.25A5.734 5.734 0 0 1 5 .005V3.25Z"></path></svg><a class="issue-link js-issue-link" data-error-text="Failed to load title" data-id="2747413371" data-permission-text="Title is private" data-url="https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/issues/45184" data-hovercard-type="pull_request" data-hovercard-url="/quarkusio/quarkus/pull/45184/hovercard" href="https://github.com/quarkusio/quarkus/pull/45184">TLS Registry: provide a TLS configuration called javax.net.ssl having truststore set in the same way as default SunJSSE provider<span class="issue-shorthand"> quarkusio/quarkus#45184</span></a></span> - still unclear whether this reaches Quarkus 3.18.0</li>
</ul>
<p dir="auto">(We need tests for all the above scenarios.)</p>
<p dir="auto">We have to decide what would the default of <code class="notranslate">quarkus.cxf.client.tls-configuration-name</code>. There are three options:</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li><code class="notranslate">default</code> pointing to <code class="notranslate">quarkus.tls.*</code></li>
<li><code class="notranslate">java.net.ssl</code></li>
<li><code class="notranslate">none</code></li>
</ul>
<p dir="auto">Any opinion on that?</p>
<p dir="auto">We have to implement and document some clean precedence rules for various levels of named and default TLS configs as well as for the old (and now deprecated) <code class="notranslate">quarkus.cxf.client."client-name".key-store*</code> and <code class="notranslate">quarkus.cxf.client."client-name".trust-store</code> family of options.</p>
<p dir="auto">I can gladly assign this to you if you like <a class="user-mention notranslate" data-hovercard-type="user" data-hovercard-url="/users/dcheng1248/hovercard" data-octo-click="hovercard-link-click" data-octo-dimensions="link_type:self" href="https://github.com/dcheng1248">@dcheng1248</a> .</p></div></div><div role="toolbar" aria-label="Reactions" data-turbolinks="false"><div class="markdown-body safe-html-box__Uwc6Al Container__ZAxOBo"><p dir="auto"><a class="user-mention notranslate" data-hovercard-type="user" data-hovercard-url="/users/ppalaga/hovercard" data-octo-click="hovercard-link-click" data-octo-dimensions="link_type:self" href="https://github.com/ppalaga">@ppalaga</a> many thanks for the detailed response and thoughts you put into this! I'm still new to large codebases so your examples provide great help for me to orient myself and think about the issue and implementations.</p>
<p dir="auto">To your question, here are my thoughts:</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li>
<p dir="auto"><code class="notranslate">default</code> pointing to <code class="notranslate">quarkus.tls.*</code>:<br>
This would introduce an additional layer of dependency on the TLS registry, although making it align with GRPC and REST could be beneficial. But would this lead to potential (security) issues? I don't understand this completely but remember seeing this in the Zulip chat between you and Clement.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p dir="auto"><code class="notranslate">java.net.ssl</code>:<br>
I believe this provides the most drop-in compatibility with URLConnectionHTTPConduit, as it doesn't require any TLS to be set and works out of the box. However, if <code class="notranslate">quarkus.tls.*</code> is set by some users, it could be confusing that the CXF default points to somewhere else entirely. Additionally, it seems that none of the existing quarkus extensions use this by default, so it would be a large deviation from existing practices.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p dir="auto">none<br>
I personally don't see much benefit to this option. Since the <code class="notranslate">quarkus.cxf.client."client-name".trust-store</code> etc. are deprecated, a named TLS configuration needs to be set anyway, and having some sort of default is potentially less work/repeated code for users than having none?</p>
</li>
</ul>
<p dir="auto">I'm tempted towards the second option just for the compatibility, but think the first could also be a good choice. Do you think it would be practical to throw an error (that asks the user to set the tls configurations) if the user sets a client-endpoint-url to https but do not have a tls configured? This would prevent going down the rabbit hole of weird server responses (like I did) due to the lack of TLS. If this is possible, I would be inclined to say the first option along with the error warning.</p>
<p dir="auto">For the implementation, I would love to contribute. How urgent do you think this issue is? I will have a lot more time starting next week, as I will be on holiday from work. This is, however, an admittedly non-trivial implementation for me (due to unfamiliarity with the codebase, needing to think about the precedence rules and my general inexperience) so it might take me a bit longer. If you prefer a faster implementation, I would love to help however I can.</p></div></div><div role="toolbar" aria-label="Reactions" class="d-flex gap-1 flex-wrap"></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></li><li data-wrapper-timeline-id="IC_kwDOElz7Sc6bUQ5R"><div class="timelineElement__l_1Q03 nonLeadingElement_inlineAvatars__g_btmU"><div data-turbolinks="false"><div class="markdown-body safe-html-box__Uwc6Al Container__ZAxOBo"><p dir="auto">I think <code class="notranslate">quarkus.tls.*</code> sounds like the best fit for storing the key for the HTTP server whereas <code class="notranslate">java.net.ssl</code> would generally make the best out of the box experience for clients that do not need mTLS. Compatibility with <code class="notranslate">URLConnectionHTTPConduit</code> is another strong argument for taking <code class="notranslate">java.net.ssl</code> as a default for <code class="notranslate">quarkus.cxf.client.tls-configuration-name</code>. So let's do it in that way.</p>
<blockquote>
<p dir="auto">Do you think it would be practical to throw an error (that asks the user to set the tls configurations) if the user sets a client-endpoint-url to https but do not have a tls configured?</p>
</blockquote>
<p dir="auto">Would we need it if we make <code class="notranslate">java.net.ssl</code> the default?</p>
<blockquote>
<p dir="auto">For the implementation, I would love to contribute. How urgent do you think this issue is?</p>
</blockquote>

data-turbolinks="false"><div class="markdown-body safe-html-box__Uwc6Al Container__ZAxOBo"><p dir="auto"><a class="user-mention notranslate" data-hovercard-type="user" data-hovercard-url="/users/ppalaga/hovercard" data-octo-click="hovercard-link-click" data-octo-dimensions="link_type:self" href="https://github.com/ppalaga">@ppalaga</a> I ran into a strange issue when testing the code. I got an exception <code class="notranslate">java.time.format.DateTimeParseException: Text cannot be parsed to a Duration</code> from your <code class="notranslate">JavaNetSslTlsBucketConfig</code> class. The relevant code is:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="    @Override
    public Duration handshakeTimeout() {
        return Duration.parse(&quot;10S&quot;);
    }"><pre class="notranslate"><code class="notranslate">    @Override
    public Duration handshakeTimeout() {
        return Duration.parse("10S");
    }
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">I had to change it (locally, I copied the class over since we're still on 3.18.2) to this for the exception to disappear.</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="    @Override
    public Duration handshakeTimeout() {
        return Duration.parse(&quot;PT10S&quot;);
    }"><pre class="notranslate"><code class="notranslate">    @Override
    public Duration handshakeTimeout() {
        return Duration.parse("PT10S");
    }
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">I think it is a local issue, given that your commit to quarkus passed CI. Any suggestions where it might have went wrong locally?</p></div></div><div role="toolbar" aria-label="Reactions" class="d-flex gap-1 flex-wrap"></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></li><li
